### Page 65

-> **Om! Salutations to the illustrious Lord Kundeshvara!** <-
<note>original: "oṃ namaḥ śrīkuṇḍeśvararāya namaḥ"; Kundeshvara is a title for Shiva meaning "Lord of the Water-Pot" or "Lord of the Pit," often associated with a famous shrine in Nepal.</note>

Through the harvest of the **Parijata tree** and others, he is truly difficult for even a king to surpass. <note>The Parijata is a celestial wishing-tree brought from the heavens.</note> Nor does the decorated poison of the gods bring any happiness here.

He does not act out of favoritism toward a woman, nor because of physical beauty. He who is completely inconsistent—that is who I am. The Lord is not able to forgive my stories. Just as power returns to me, how does consciousness become uneven when facing those who are powerful?

Let the world-consuming ones be imagined for me. How is he, who is established in the Self, not held by the leaders? Like a body, he holds the moon in order to create. That god is indeed not easily attainable.

Having sat down, do not depart! The consciousness of men is colored by passion. How does pride and its ilk create the "I" of those intoxicated by ego? Indeed, in this <term>Samsara</term> <note>original: "saṃsṛtau"; the beginningless cycle of birth, death, and rebirth</note>, he is imagined—what does he not do? Then, he does what is appropriate together with her. Oh, the cycle of existence for men is truly a delusion!

He knows everything, yet who would imagine this very difficult path? That path is right here, entirely delicate. Now, for men, this path is very extensive and wide. Truly, the <term>Dharma</term> <note>original: "dharmma"; refers to righteousness, duty, and the cosmic order</note> that has been accumulated is of the nature of doubt.

By whatever rule he approaches righteousness, so the <term>Marga</term> <note>original: "mārgaḥ"; the spiritual path or way</note> creates righteousness. Righteousness does not act for men alone. He truly enters the completion of righteousness, established in the rule of virtue.

He has now dismissed doubt in his heart, just as it creates the form of doubt; how could he not teach the story of righteousness?

<summary>This page contains a philosophical poem or treatise discussing the difficulty of the spiritual path (Marga) and the nature of worldly delusion (Samsara), beginning with a Hindu salutation to Lord Kundeshvara.</summary>

--- Page 132 ---
<meta>Continuity Note: The text continues from the previous page's discussion on the "appropriate path" and "consciousness." The final sentence of the previous page ("That path is right here...") leads directly into the opening of this page, which identifies that path with the realization of the Self (Atman) through consciousness (Samvida).</meta>

### Folio 65, Reverse

Together with **consciousness**. <note>original: "saṃvidā sārdhaṃ"; samvida refers to a deep, meditative state of awareness or knowledge.</note> As it is heard: "It does not reach the wood." <note>This likely refers to a metaphor where consciousness, unlike fire or physical tools, does not depend on a physical fuel or substrate like wood.</note> For that consciousness is never withdrawn by any other means of movement; he himself, directly, is not contradicted—he is the **Self**. Or, through the abbreviation of cessation, he binds it together as it is near. This is the **Self**.

For all things are perceived by the force of logic; however, that very **Self** is not composite. <note>In Indian philosophy, something "composite" (samhata) is made of parts and will eventually fall apart. The Self is argued to be non-composite and eternal.</note> For even while existing, he does not exceed his own being. For thus this **Self** stands only by being unified. The **Self** is not merely a collection of things, because it exceeds its individual parts. And that **Self** is well-known—it is that which is established.

It is the consciousness of the **Self**. Thus, what is seen is the **Self**; by metaphor, it is indicated by its foundation. Here it is said: there is no doubt regarding the **Self** in this way. Nor is there any disagreement for anyone here. Indeed, there is no contradiction in the **Self**. It is well-known to everyone.

For this is not "unproven" like a **pot** or a **cloth**. <note>The "pot" (ghata) and "cloth" (pata) are the most common examples in Indian logic to represent external, physical objects that require eyes and light to be known. The author argues the Self is different because it is self-shining.</note> By this, there is such a connection. This is not unproven like some remote or indirect object. This is the **Self**. Thus, through unswerving **consciousness** <term>Vijnana</term> <note>original: "vijñānena"; specifically referring to the discriminating faculty of the mind or pure awareness.</note>, it is indeed well-known. Indeed, something that is disputed cannot be called "well-known."

No one perceives a disputed **Self**. Thus, it is simply established. Now, as for what was said—that "the **Self** is only that"—that is incorrect. Even there, it is the same: he alone is designated. As the **Self** is well-known, is there any contradictory understanding found there? That has already been addressed. For he is well-known everywhere. He perceives the **Self** in this way. Now, this is incorrect: "The Self is well-known by itself." For there is nothing in the world not well-known, like a **pot** and so on which are well-understood. As the **Self** is well-known...

And how is that again well-known? Just as one knows a **pot**, or as a **pot** appears, so it is—it shines forth as it is. In that much alone, it is established. Thus the **Self** shines forth. Therefore, the **Self** is indeed well-known; there is no contradiction here.

<summary>This page presents a philosophical argument for the self-evident nature of the Atman (Self), arguing that unlike physical objects like pots or cloth, the Self requires no external proof because it is the foundation of consciousness itself.</summary>
<keywords>Atman, Self, Consciousness, Vijnana, Epistemology, Indian Logic, Self-evidence, Prasiddha</keywords>

--- Page 133 ---
<meta>Continuity Note: This page follows the discussion of the Self (Atman) and its relationship to consciousness (Samvida). The previous page established that the Self is non-composite and self-evident, unlike physical objects. This page expands on the states of consciousness—waking, dreaming, and deep sleep—using the metaphor of a mirror to explain how the world appears within the mind.</meta>

### Folio 66

Having reached that state, it is just as if a person named **Devadatta**, <note>original: "devadattaḥ"; a generic name used in Indian logic, similar to "John Doe" in English.</note> while asleep, opens his eyes to himself but remains unable to sleep. Or, perhaps there is another person who is also asleep, but due to the constant experience of pleasure and pain, he does not wake up; he is eternal, for he sleeps eternally.

Is there **distraction**, <term>Vikṣepa: the mental state of being scattered, or the cosmic power that projects the appearance of the world.</term> or is there no cessation of **distraction**? In that state, no **distraction** is even possible. Just as a **mirror** <note>original: "mukura"</note> does not cease to exist simply because a face and light are present, so too, when **consciousness** is present, neither **distraction** nor the calming of **distraction** is truly possible. Otherwise, there would be no experience of sleep, nor any awakening into the waking state. Just as a dream is not something separate from **consciousness**,

it becomes one with it. This meaning shines forth by itself through the mind, just as a dream appears within **consciousness**. Like the way **distraction** finds its rest within **distraction**, or as the reflection of a face appears in a **mirror**, so the appearance of the world exists within **consciousness**. The **distraction** of **consciousness** is not something additional to **consciousness** itself; for **consciousness** itself takes on the nature of **distraction**. As it is projected or scattered, so it appears.

Whether there is awakening or not, the removal of **distraction** is not possible as a separate event. The appearances of the **waking**, **dreaming**, and **deep sleep** <note>original: "jāgrat svapna suṣuptīnāṃ"; these are the three standard states of consciousness in Indian philosophy.</note> states are diverse. Since these always shine within **consciousness**, **distraction** appears in those various ways. Just as the reflection of a face is not something separate from the **mirror**, the **distraction** of **consciousness** is not something separate from **consciousness** itself. It is like this:

**Distraction**. Just as the Self, as **consciousness**, opens itself while seemingly asleep, so the Self, as **distraction**, shines by its own power. For **consciousness** itself is the awakening into the form of the world. Just as **consciousness** is not different from **distraction**, so it is not separate from the form of the world. Indeed, **consciousness** itself vibrates as the **manifold world**. <term>Prapañca: the "expansion" or "multiplicity" of the physical universe.</term>

Thus, **consciousness** alone appears in the form of **distraction**. For if one were to "wipe away" **distraction**, **consciousness** would not remain as a separate thing. Just as it does not remain by wiping away **distraction**, so **consciousness** does not remain by wiping away the **manifold world**. In whatever way it is projected, in that way it appears. It is because of that very **consciousness** that the appearance of **distraction** exists.

<summary>This page explores the Advaita (non-dual) philosophy that the world and its distractions are not separate from pure consciousness, using the metaphor of a mirror and its reflection. It argues that the states of waking, dreaming, and deep sleep are all internal manifestations of a single, underlying awareness.</summary>
<keywords>Devadatta, consciousness (Samvit), distraction (Vikshepa), mirror metaphor (Mukura), waking (Jagrat), dreaming (Svapna), deep sleep (Sushupti), manifold world (Prapancha)</keywords>

### Folio 80

->Salutations to Ganesha.<- <note>original: "śrī gaṇeśāya namaḥ"</note> Just as the **staff** <term>Danda: literally a "stick" or "staff," symbolizing the authority of an ascetic or the central axis of the cosmos.</term> came into being, so did that Lord **Hari** <note>A name for Vishnu.</note> of the beautiful, slender waist. Meditating fully with the **mind as the kindling**, <note>original: "manasā samidhā"; samidha is the wood used to feed a sacred sacrificial fire.</note> he indeed... || 1 ||

**Just as** the fire is held in the hands of the one wearing a loincloth, or resides in the home in a threefold way, so the **staff** arose, being held by that Lord **Hari** of the beautiful, slender waist. Meditating fully with the **mind as the kindling**, he indeed...

**The Rudra-like ones** <note>original: "rudrādikā"; referring to the storm-gods or forms of Shiva.</note> speak of these as fires, and they are also called **measures**. <term>Matra: a unit of measurement, often referring to the musical beats or the components of the sacred syllable Om.</term> Just as the mantras are themselves fires, so it is everywhere and at all times. Whoever the Lord Fire is, he is truly the **mind as the kindling**.

**The fires arose.** Then the gods are said to be those of the mind. He truly speaks: Whoever the Lord Fire is, he is truly the **mind as the kindling**. Just as the mantras are fires, so it is everywhere.

**As** those Rudra-like ones and others are called **measures**. Just as the mantras are themselves fires, so it is always and everywhere. Whoever the Lord Fire is, he is truly the **mind as the kindling**.

**In form**... they are the clouds; remember them always. They speak of the Rudra-like fires, and these are called **measures**. In this way, all the fires are described exactly as they are.

**Consisting of this** <note>original: "yanmayī"</note> knowledge, this teaching is said to destroy all sins. One should meditate on the triad through which the fire of movement is established. These are the fires. Thus, the gods conversed regarding the **staff** with the **wise one**. <note>original: "sumedhasā"; one of great intellect or wisdom.</note>

<summary>This page presents a meditative liturgy equating the ritual fire (Agni) with the human mind and mantras. It uses the "staff" (Danda) and the deity Vishnu (Hari) to symbolize the manifestation of spiritual authority and the internalizing of Vedic sacrifice.</summary>

<meta>Continuity Note: This leaf transitions from the previous folio's esoteric meditation on the internal "sun" and "youthful mind" into a formal devotional hymn known as the Guru Stotram (the Hymn to the Teacher). ->**Salutations to the Holy Guru. Om, salutations to Shiva.**<-

1. Salutations to that **Holy Guru** <note>original: "Śrīgurave"; a title of high respect for the spiritual preceptor.</note> who, with the application of the medicinal ointment of knowledge, has opened the eyes of one blinded by the darkness of ignorance. <term>Jnana-anjana-shalaka: a small wand or "stick" used in ancient medicine to apply healing salve to the eyes; here, it is a metaphor for the Guru removing spiritual cataracts.</term>

2. Salutations to that Holy Guru who has revealed the state of reality that pervades the entire universe of moving and unmoving things in one **unbroken, circular form**. <note>original: "akhanda-mandalakaram"; refers to the infinite, undivided nature of the ultimate reality (Brahman).</note>

3. Salutations to that Holy Guru who, by bestowing the knowledge of the Self, burns away the **bonds of karma** <note>The cycle of cause and effect resulting from actions.</note> accumulated through many previous lifetimes.

4. Salutations to that Holy Guru who revealed that state of being which pervades everything—whether stationary or moving, and all that exists in between.

5. Salutations to that Holy Guru who revealed the state of **pure consciousness** <note>original: "chinmayam"</note> that pervades the **three worlds** <term>Trailokya: in Hindu cosmology, these are the earth, the atmosphere (or heaven), and the celestial/spiritual realms.</term> and everything within them, both animate and inanimate.

6. Salutations to that Holy Guru whose lotus feet are adorned by the crest-jewels of all the **Scriptures**, <note>original: "śruti"; literally "that which is heard," referring to the Vedas.</note> and who acts as the sun that causes the lotus of **Vedanta** <term>Vedanta: the philosophical end-goal of the Vedas, focusing on the unity of the individual soul and the universal spirit.</term> to blossom.

7. Salutations to that Holy Guru who is pure consciousness itself, eternal, peaceful, existing beyond the reaches of space, untainted, and transcending the subtle elements of sound and form. <note>original: "bindu-nada-kala-atitah"; a reference to the Guru being beyond the three stages of cosmic manifestation in Tantric thought.</note>

<summary>This page contains the opening seven verses of the Guru Stotram, a foundational hymn of praise that identifies the spiritual teacher as the ultimate source of enlightenment and the destroyer of ignorance. The text describes the Guru not just as a human teacher, but as a cosmic principle that pervades the entire universe.</summary>

...the Scriptures. 

He worshipped the **Teacher of the World** <note>original: "bhuvana gurum"; referring to the Buddha.</note> daily, like the flowing waters of the Ganges. Where millions of images of the Buddhas were fashioned above with golden plates, there this temple, a **Jewel Stupa**, was constructed of gold. In that place, the worship of the Stupa <term>Chaitya: a Buddhist shrine or monument, often containing relics or marking a sacred spot.</term> makes the **Eightfold Path** <note>The fundamental Buddhist path to liberation: right view, resolve, speech, action, livelihood, effort, mindfulness, and concentration.</note> bear fruit.

In the excellent city of the **Sugata** <term>Sugata: "The One who has Gone Well"; a common epithet for the Buddha.</term>, through the vehicles of the Path of the Buddha's Law, he is famous throughout all the worlds. Humans and gods alike worship the Buddha. Whatever merit has been achieved in the Buddha-realm by the excellent **Sons of the Sugata** <note>original: "sugata suta"; a term for Bodhisattvas, those on the path to Buddhahood.</note> throughout the whole world, through that, the pure teaching of the Dharma-Buddha has protected the world.

**Bowing down.**

**Thus have I heard:** At one time, the **Blessed One** <note>original: "Bhagavan"</note> was staying in the city of **Shravasti**, in the **Jetavana** grove, in the garden of **Anathapindada** <note>A wealthy patron famous for his generosity, who purchased this grove for the Buddha by covering the ground with gold coins.</note>, together with a great community of monks—about five hundred monks—and with a great host of **Bodhisattvas** <term>Bodhisattva: a being who seeks enlightenment for the sake of all living creatures.</term>,

great beings, and many **Sons of the Gods**. Then, the Blessed One, desiring to reveal the discourse of the Law named the "Glory of the Dharma" for the benefit and happiness of all beings to be disciplined, spoke the **One Hundred and Eight Names** of the **Wisdom-Being** <note>original: "Jnanasattva"; often associated with Manjushri, the Bodhisattva of Wisdom.</note>,

which is radiant as if made of great jewels. Then, addressing all those beings who were to be disciplined, he said: "Listen, O **Sons of Noble Family**, to the essence of the qualities of the Buddha's Dharma. By chanting this, all inauspiciousness is destroyed and all sins are averted."

**One should chant this Great Mantra:**

**Om! Salutations to the Blessed One, the Diamond-Being, the One Thus-Gone, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Awakened One.** <note>original: "Om namo bhagavate vajra satvaya tathagataya arhate samyaksambuddhaya"</note>

**As follows: Om! Diamond-Being! Diamond-Being! Diamond-Being! Made of Diamond! Shatterer of the Diamond-Heart! Diamond...** <term>Vajra: "Diamond" or "Thunderbolt"; symbolizes the indestructible and irresistible nature of ultimate reality and the power of enlightenment.</term>

--- Page 138 ---
<meta>This page marks a significant thematic shift from the previous page's Buddhist context (the Buddha and the Jetavana grove) into a Shaivite discourse focusing on the laws of Karma and the grace of Shiva. It addresses the philosophical problem of why some are born into high or low status, attributing these outcomes to past actions and devotion to the Great Lord.</meta>

...one does not attain [liberation] otherwise; he is the maker and the non-maker of his own pleasure and pain. In this way, the fruits of **actions** <term>Karma: the law of cause and effect where one’s deeds determine their future state.</term> are experienced, whether they be **well-performed** <note>original: "sukrita"</note> or **evil-deeds** <note>original: "dushkrita"</note>. By the grace of **Shiva**, all **perfection** <term>Siddhi: spiritual or supernatural attainment or success.</term> indeed becomes firm and unmoving. If, through his grace, lordship over all the worlds is attained, for whom then is **complete success** <note>original: "samsiddhi"</note> very difficult to obtain? 

In this way, actions are experienced, whether they be well-performed or evil-deeds. Concerning this doubt, O **Great Being** <note>original: "Mahasattva"; often used for a Bodhisattva in Buddhist texts, here applied in a Shaivite context.</note>, one should remain devoted to the worship of Shiva. For there are auspicious and inauspicious actions, and whatever pleasures and pains exist—how can one understand the nature of **Righteousness and Unrighteousness** <term>Dharma and Adharma: the moral order of the universe and its violation.</term> across the twenty-eight [realms/states]? Some beings are born with deformed bodies, while others possess [beauty]. Who indeed is wise enough to explain the true nature of Dharma and Adharma? How is a living creature born, and how does it die again? In this way, the Lord of Sages asked many kinds of questions.

As I have stated previously, there is nothing higher than **Righteousness** <note>original: "Dharma"</note>. If happiness in this world comes from Righteousness and suffering comes only from unrighteousness, then how can a **low-born person** <term>Shudra: historically the fourth social class, often excluded from royal power in traditional texts.</term> become a King? How can a **Priest** <term>Vipra: a Brahmin or member of the priestly class.</term> become extremely lowly? How does the daughter of a wealthy lord fall into a state of slavery? 

All **merit** <term>Punya: spiritual "currency" or luck earned through virtuous acts.</term> belongs to the one who always worships the Lord in images and other forms. Such a person is born in the world as a King, a subduer of all living beings. One who performs the **Great Gift** <term>Mahadana: a ritualized, significant act of charity.</term>, especially the **Gift of Land** <note>original: "bhumidanam"; traditionally considered one of the highest forms of merit-making.</note>, is born as a person of great wisdom and a master of all sciences. 

Fortunate, worth seeing, beautiful, and free from all sickness and anxiety is he who practices great devotion, being dedicated to the **Great Lord** <term>Maheshvara: an epithet of Shiva meaning "Great Lord."</term>. He attains all this that I have previously proclaimed. Abandoning all suffering, he is born with very great strength. He is born as a great [being]... he is born liberated from all sins, of this there is no doubt. He shall attain the **Supreme Place** <note>original: "param sthanam"; referring to the highest heaven or liberation.</note>, having gone where one no longer grieves.

All this has been told by me to you, O best of sages. Whoever reads this or even hears it, attains the supreme goal.

<summary>This page discusses the mechanics of Karma, explaining that social status and physical form result from past deeds, while emphasizing that devotion to Shiva and acts like the "Great Gift" of land can overcome suffering and lead to the supreme state.</summary>

### The Rite of Protection
<note>original: "Raksha Vidhi"; a ritual performed to create a spiritual shield around the practitioner</note>

Now begins the protection. Having consecrated the **Aghora Mantra** <note>original: "Aghora-mantram"; a powerful invocation to Shiva in his "non-terrifying" yet potent form</note> seven times, one should perform the protection. Having bound the hair-tuft <note>shikha; the lock of hair at the crown of the head, tied to seal one's energy</note> and performed the "binding of the directions" <note>digbandhanam; a ritual gesture to close off the ten directions from negative influences</note> on all sides.

Having formed the **Thumb-Gesture** <note>original: "Angustha-mudram"; a specific hand position used in Tantric worship</note> on the fingers. In both hands.

#### The Mantra:
**Om! Salutations to the Blessed Lord Rudra, the Non-Terrifying, the Non-Terrifying in form, the Non-Terrifying and Terrifying in form! To the Fierce and more-than-Fierce in form! To the Destroyer of all obstacles!**

**For the total annihilation of all ghosts, vampires, nature spirits, demons, female spirits, witches, planetary spirits, malevolent spirits, and hungry ghosts** <note>original: "bhuta-vetala-yaksha-rakshasa-shakini-dakini-graha-pishacha-preta"; a comprehensive list of supernatural entities in Hindu demonology</note>. **Uproot, uproot my enemies! Cut, cut the magic of others!**

**With the Aghora Mantra, provide, provide protection on all sides! Make, make my body into an adamantine body** <note>original: "vajrakaya"; a body as strong as a diamond or a thunderbolt, immune to harm</note>.

**Hram Hrim Hrum Hraim Hraum Hrah!** <note>these are "Bija" or seed-syllables which have no literal translation but are believed to carry specific vibrational powers</note> **To the Thunderbolt, Hail!** <note>original: "Vajraya Svaha"</note>

Thus concludes the Method for Universal Protection using the Aghora Mantra.

### Practical Applications
Now, the practical applications. Having taken the wood of a **Great Tree** <note>Mahavriksha</note> or a **Peacock-Tree** <note>Mayurasya kashtham; possibly referring to the Albizia lebbeck or a tree associated with peacock-like patterns</note>, and having consecrated it one hundred and eight times, whoever's head it is tied upon becomes subjugated <note>vashyo; brought under the influence or charm of the practitioner</note>.

### The Mantra for Subjugation
**Om Shrim Hrim Klim Blum! To Chamunda** <note>a fearsome form of the Mother Goddess</note>, **Vicche! Bring, bring the entire world under my control! Do it, do it! Hail!**

By chanting this one hundred and eight times, one becomes successful <note>siddho; achieving the power or "siddhi" associated with the mantra</note.

**Incense:** One should use ghee, honey, and Guggulu resin <note>a fragrant resin used in Vedic and Buddhist medicine/ritual</note> from the **Sriparna tree** <note>Gmelina arborea</note>...

<meta>This page transitions from the practical application of the Aghora Mantra for protection (detailed on the previous page) into a deeper philosophical and cosmological explanation of the ritual’s origins. # The Discourse on the Divine Aperture

Now begins the instruction regarding the placement of the assembly members <note>original: "sadasyam abhyasam"; this refers to the meditative placement of ritual participants or divine entities within the practitioner's consciousness</note>. From the channels of the senses, Fire was born. Fire perceives; in this way, it belongs to Fire. The creation of the **Life-breath** <note>original: "prana-srishti"</note> also occurs right here.

The Sages spoke the Word <note>original: "vacham"; the personified goddess of speech and sacred sound</note> within the lightning. How does that which becomes manifest, manifest? Through that, everything comes from him. Just as here, this very one is the **Lord of Progeny** <note>original: "Prajapati"; the primordial creator deity in Vedic tradition</note>. When he thinks, "This is to be eaten within me," he then seeks it out. 

No one indeed knows this from the Mind <note>original: "manasa"</note> or the Life-breath. From Knowledge <note>original: "vidya" </note>, he then covers the mind. He becomes the Lord of Progeny, thinking, "This is mine." 

### The Secret of the Opening

Just as humans know the **opening** <note>original: "randhra"; literally a hole or aperture, but here referring to a mystical weak point or a gateway between the human and divine realms</note> of a god, even so is this opening of the divine found in knowledge. With the eye, the gods saw this opening. Then, he closes this opening. That indeed becomes the food. "This is to be eaten within me," he thinks.

Just as humans know the **opening** of a god, with the eye, the gods saw this opening. Then, he closes this opening. That indeed becomes the food. "This is to be eaten within me," he thinks.

Just as humans know the **opening** of a god, with the eye, the gods saw this opening. Then, he closes this opening. That indeed becomes the food. "This is to be eaten within me," he <unclear>thinks.</unclear> <note>The repetition in these final paragraphs suggests a liturgical chant or a meditative formula intended to be recited multiple times to reinforce the practitioner's understanding of the divine aperture.</note>

# The Perfection of Wisdom of the Kingdom of Dharma

Now. **Salutations to the Buddha.** To the **King of the Dharma**, the **Buddha**, the **One Who Has Gone Well** <note>original: "Sugata"; a common epithet for a Buddha, meaning one who has attained a state of perfect bliss</note>, the **Liberated One**. To Wisdom <unclear>salutations to</unclear> Wisdom... this is the Law <note>original: "dharma"</note>. <unclear>Now</unclear>, the **Perfection of Wisdom** <note>original: "Prajnaparamita"; the personification of the highest transcendental wisdom in Mahayana Buddhism</note> of the Kingdom of Dharma. Salutations to the **Blessed Lady**, the Perfection of Wisdom.

She whose face is like the **full moon**. Who <unclear>within Wisdom</unclear> dwells in the highest excellence. She is the **Retaining Incantation** <note>original: "dharani"; a sacred sequence of syllables used to "retain" or hold the power of a Buddhist teaching in the mind</note> that provides clear wisdom. Since she makes all the most meritorious laws easy to accomplish, the giver of boons... she who is the perfect clarity... to the one who has reached the limit...

For the sake of spreading the excellent **Dharma of the Buddha**, one should realize Wisdom. The Wisdom of the Law of the Buddha, the vast Wisdom, the excellent and pure Wisdom. She is the giver of the spreading of the essence of the Law. 

The virtuous mind that holds the essence, the giver of the excellent Law of the Buddha. This is the sacred union of the excellent Wisdom of the Law. <unclear>Let it be accomplished.</unclear>

### The Sacred Incantations

**The Mantra:**
Om! O meritorious one of the Wisdom of the Law, giver of all ease, the mantra of the covenant. To the Wisdom that makes all things easy, the mantra of excellent wisdom, the giver of the excellent Law, the possessor of the mantra. The giver of the excellent **Wheel of the Law** <note>original: "Dharma-chakra"; the symbol of the Buddha's teaching and the turning of the cosmic order</note>, the mantra of the good Law.

**The Mantra:**
Om! O mind of the Law, O good mind of the Law, O good union, O excellent Law, O giver of the good Law, O union. The mantra of the good Law, the good union, the giver of ease, she who possesses the mind of the good Law. The union of the **Universal Mantra**, <unclear>let it be</unclear>, she who possesses the mind of the good Law.

**The Mantra:**
Om! O giver of the mind of the Law, O good union, O giver of boons, O giver of the excellent Law, O possessor of the good mind. The mantra of ease, the mantra of ease, the giver of the Law.

--- Page 146 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the Mahayana Buddhist praise of Prajnaparamita seen on the previous folio to a Hindu Puranic description of Vaikuntha, the abode of Vishnu. This shift is characteristic of Nepalese "Mulapatra" or composite manuscripts, where various protective hymns (stotras) and descriptions of heavens from both Buddhist and Hindu traditions were collected in a single volume for the patron’s merit.</meta>

The city named **Vaikuntha** <note>the celestial abode of the god Vishnu</note> is the <unclear>supreme refuge</unclear> in all four directions. All the hosts of the gods and demons, the **Siddhas** <note>perfected celestial beings</note>, and the **Sadhyas** <note>a group of minor Vedic deities</note> are always there. Where the gods, always disciplined, the best of beings, play eternally.

There the gods dwell, even those who take on human form. They are all great lords of the gods, as well as the auspicious **Rishis** <note>inspired sages or seers</note>. The great-souled ones are settled there, those who are always devoted to **Dharma** <note>righteousness and cosmic order</note>. Where indeed

all the hosts of **Gandharvas** <note>celestial musicians</note> and the hosts of **Apsaras** <note>celestial nymphs</note> dwell. They play with great enthusiasm, as do the auspicious Rishis. The Gandharvas and the Apsaras have entered here. All

of these are happy there, with the fulfillment of all their desires. Where indeed dwell those men who are distinguished by virtuous marks. All the **Munis** <note>silent sages or ascetics</note> there have burned away their sins through austerity. Together with **Brahma** <note>the creator deity</note>,

all are present where the gods dwell along with **Indra** <note>original: "Vasava"; the king of the gods</note>. The Rishis and the Siddhas, and others who are seekers of the Ultimate Truth <note>original: "Brahmavadins"; those who discourse upon the nature of the Supreme Reality</note>. These indeed dwell where the **Lord Janardana** <note>an epithet of Vishnu, meaning "the Agitator of Men" or "He who is sought by the people"</note> resides.

Through that very great action, they are endowed with the knowledge of Dharma. Where indeed dwell those men of auspicious signs. Where indeed dwell those great-souled ones who have conquered their senses.

<summary>This page provides a devotional description of Vaikuntha, the celestial realm of Vishnu (Janardana), depicting it as a place of eternal joy inhabited by gods, sages, and those who have purified themselves through austerity.</summary>

One may eat any other edible things as they occur. <insert>Here are the names of the five edible things.</insert> The five-clawed animals <note>original: "pancha nakha"</note> that are edible are: the hare, the porcupine, the tortoise, the hedgehog, and the monitor lizard. Whatever those five-clawed animals are, one may eat them. One should know there is no fault in this; one is released from all sins.

ॐ Salutations to <term>Vinayaka</term> <note>original: "Vinayakaya"; another name for Ganesha, the elephant-headed god who removes obstacles and is traditionally invoked first.</note> || Salutations to the Sun || Salutations to <term>Shiva</term> <note>The destroyer and transformer within the Hindu trinity.</note> || Salutations to <term>Narayana</term> <note>A name for Vishnu, the sustainer of the universe.</note> || Salutations to <term>Saraswati</term> <note>The goddess of knowledge, music, and the arts.</note> || Salutations to <term>Kamadeva</term> <note>The god of love and desire.</note> || Salutations to the venerable Teacher || Salutations to all the gods || Salutations to the family deities || Salutations to the personal chosen deities ||

Of this sacred <term>Navakshari Vidya Mantra</term> <note>original: "Navakshari-vidya-mantra"; the "Nine-Syllable Knowledge Mantra," also known as the Navarna Mantra, dedicated to the supreme Goddess.</note>, the Seer is <term>Brahma</term> <note>The creator god who first "saw" or received the mantra.</note>, the poetic meter is <term>Gayatri</term>, and the deities are the Great Kali, the Great Lakshmi, and the Great Saraswati || The "seed" syllable is *Aim* || The power is *Hrim* || The "pin" is *Klim* <note>original: "Kilaka"; a syllable that acts as a lock or focal point for the mantra's energy.</note> ||

The ritual application <note>original: "viniyoga"</note> of this chanting is for the fulfillment of all my heart's desires || Now follows the **Hand-Placement** <note>original: "Karanyasa"; a ritual where the practitioner touches parts of the hand while reciting syllables to "place" the deity's power into their body.</note> || ॐ *Aim*, salutations to the two thumbs || ॐ *Hrim*, salutations to the two index fingers || ॐ *Klim*, salutations to the two middle fingers || ॐ *Chamundayai*, salutations to the two ring fingers ||

In this same way, the placement of mantras on the heart and other limbs is performed || Now for the **Meditation** <note>original: "Dhyanam"; a descriptive verse used to visualize the deity before chanting.</note> || I bow to the goddess <term>Chamunda</term> <note>A fierce, protective form of the Divine Mother.</note>, the crusher of the demon Munda; she resembles a heap of blue eye-ointment, carries a trident, a discus, and a mace, and her face bears a gentle smile || Thus ends the meditation ||

**ॐ Aim Hrim Klim Chamundayai Vicche**

**The Meaning of the Mantra:**
The syllable *Aim* is the very form of creation; the syllable *Hrim* is the sustainer of existence || The syllable *Klim* is the form of desire, and Chamunda is the crusher of the demon Munda ||
The syllable *Yai* is the eternal giver of boons, and *Vicche* is the granter of fearlessness || Thus, this Nine-Syllable Knowledge bestows all perfections ||

Thus ends the **Hymn of the Nine-Syllable Mantra** || May there be auspiciousness || <unclear>॥</unclear>

## Om! <meta>While the previous page concluded a general invocation to Vāsudeva and the sacrificial fire, this page (22) transitions into a specific technical treatise on Mantra-śāstra (the science of sacred sounds). The text shifts from Vedic/Puranic ritualism to a more structured classification of mantras, attributed to a specific author, Madhavamukha. The central binding hole noted in the OCR suggests this is a manuscript leaf where the text flows across the physical gap.</meta>

<margin>Auspiciousness to Shiva!</margin>

|| **May there be well-being!** ||
Summarized and compiled here is the entirety of the **Science of Mantras** <term>mantra-śāstra; the systematic study of sacred sounds and their applications</term> by **Madhavamukha**, the son of **Harihara**, who is a great poet and a man of great wisdom, deeply proficient in the Great Tantras of the Upanishads. To the Blessed Aryaman <note>original: "Aryamṇaḥ"; a Vedic deity often associated with the sun and the rules of society.</note>—

As it was said, the **threefold nature of mantras** <term>traividhya; the state of being three-pronged</term> consists of three types, based on the division into the qualities of **Purity** (*Sattvic*), **Passion** (*Rajasic*), and **Darkness** (*Tamasic*). Mantras categorized as **Pure** are those chanted regularly.

Those which are employed at all times <note>Meaning the daily "obligatory" rites performed without a specific selfish motive.</note> are proclaimed as **Pure**. **Passional** mantras are for those who desire specific rewards or fruits.

In my considered opinion regarding the examination of mantras, three forms will be possible; I shall explain the mantras just as they have been heard <note>original: "yathā-śrutaṃ"; indicating the author is following oral tradition or scriptural transmission.</note>.
Thus, the threefold nature of mantras should always be known by the wise. The threefold nature of mantras is spoken of exactly as it is heard from the scriptures.

<column-break/>

—bowing down to **Shiva** for the attainment of all desired goals, I speak. Thus, the matter is concisely compiled. This path is always approved. In that regard, the threefold nature of mantras is now described.

And mantras, when properly consecrated, attain success. They are known to be of three types based on the distinction of their own inherent qualities <term>guṇa; the three fundamental attributes of nature: balance, activity, and inertia</term>.

As the mantra is, so is the success for men; this is certain. Through **Pure** mantras, success aimed at **liberation** <term>moksha; release from the cycle of birth and death</term> certainly prevails.

Those performed with a specific goal or result in view are remembered as **Passional**. However, those used in cruel or harmful acts <note>original: "krūra-karmaṇi"; refers to "black magic" or aggressive tantric rites meant to subdue or harm.</note> are remembered as **Dark**.

I declare in brief what was spoken by **Shiva** and the other gods. The wise know the best mantra to be that which is composed of three syllables <note>original: "tribhir varṇaiḥ"; can also mean "of three colors" or "of three classes." In this context, it likely refers to the three qualities mentioned above or the sacred syllable Om which contains three sounds.</note>.

<summary>This page introduces a summary of "Mantra-śāstra" (the science of mantras) by the author Madhavamukha, categorizing all sacred chants into three types—Sattvic (pure/liberating), Rajasic (result-oriented), and Tamasic (aggressive/cruel)—based on their intent and the quality of the practitioner.</summary>

<margin>Auspiciousness to Shiva!</margin>

Summarized and compiled here is the entirety of the **Science of Mantras** <term>mantra-śāstra; the systematic study of sacred sounds and their spiritual applications</term> by **Madhavamukha**, the son of **Harihara**. He is a great poet and a man of great wisdom, deeply proficient in the Great Tantras <note>Esoteric ritual or philosophical texts.</note> of the Upanishads. To the Blessed Aryaman <note>original: "Aryamṇaḥ"; a Vedic deity associated with the sun, social order, and nobility.</note>—

As it was said, the **threefold nature of mantras** <term>traividhya; the state of being divided into three distinct categories</term> consists of three types, based on the division into the qualities of **Purity** (*Sattvic*), **Passion** (*Rajasic*), and **Darkness** (*Tamasic*). Mantras categorized as **Pure** are those chanted regularly as part of one's spiritual discipline.

Those which are employed at all times <note>This refers to the "nitya" or obligatory daily rites performed as a duty without a specific selfish motive.</note> are proclaimed as **Pure**. **Passional** mantras are for those who desire specific rewards or "fruits" from their recitation.

In my considered opinion regarding the examination of mantras, three forms will be possible; I shall explain the mantras just as they have been heard <note>original: "yathā-śrutaṃ"; indicating the author is following the traditional oral transmission or scriptural authority.</note>.

Thus, the threefold nature of mantras should always be known by the wise. The threefold nature of mantras is spoken of exactly as it is heard from the scriptures.

<summary>This leaf introduces a treatise by Madhavamukha on "Mantra-shastra," categorizing sacred sounds into three types—Pure, Passional, and Dark—based on the practitioner's motivation and the qualities of nature.</summary>
<keywords>mantra-shastra, Madhavamukha, Sattvic, Rajasic, Tamasic, Aryaman, Sanskrit manuscript, British Museum collection</keywords>
